.
The triangles are not affected since there is no pair involved in the diagram, so the corollary follows.
∎

Consider
P
a finite poset.
For a fixed
q\in P
, Cor.
4.7
implies that the number of parallelogram diagrams needed to check is
d(P)\cdot|P|
where
d(P)
is the maximum number of predecessors of any element in
P
.
This implies that the running time for computing
L(\varphi,\psi)
in the case of a finite poset
P
and
\mathcal{C}
a
FC
category is

O\left(|P|\;d(P)\;N\;log(|P|)\right)

where
N=\max_{p\in P}\{|F(P)|,|G(P)|\}

and for
\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{Vec}_{\mathbb{F}}
is

O\left(|P|\;d(P)\;N^{3}\;log(|P|)\right)

where
N^{3}=\max_{p\prec q\in P}\{\rk F([p\prec q],\rk G([p\prec q],\rk\varphi_{p},\rk\psi_{p}\}
recalling that ny linear transformations,
h,h^{\prime}:V\to W
, we have that
\rk(h-h^{\prime})\leq\rk(v)+\rk(v^{\prime})
.

Note that this number of diagrams to check cannot be further improved.
In general,
p<q<r
in
P
, lack of commutativity of assignments mean that
L_{\bigtriangledown}^{p}(\varphi,\psi)
and
L_{\bigtriangledown}^{q}(\varphi,\psi)
do not bound each other, unless we have some additional conditions like those in Lemma
4.6
.
Similarly
L_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.93002pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}

do not bound each other.
This can be seen in the following example.

Example 4.8
.

We continue with Example
3.11
using the flow
\mathcal{T}
given by
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}=m+\lfloor\varepsilon\rfloor
.
We take the same
F
along with
\hat{G}
(a modification of
G
from that example) in
\mathrm{Set}^{\mathbb{N}}
given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F:}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a_{4}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a_{5}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b^{\prime}_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b^{\prime}_{4}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\hat{G}:}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{4}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{5}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c_{i}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c^{\prime}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{c^{\prime}_{3}}}

The distance between the points in their respective sets are:

\begin{matrix}d_{0}^{F}(a_{0},b_{0})=3&d_{1}^{F}(a_{1},b_{1})=2&d_{2}^{F}(a_{2},b_{2})=1&d_{3}^{\hat{G}}(b^{\prime}_{3},c_{3})=2\\
&&d_{2}^{\hat{G}}(c_{2},c^{\prime}_{2})=2&d_{3}^{\hat{G}}(b^{\prime}_{3},c_{3}^{\prime})=2&d_{4}^{\hat{G}}(b^{\prime}_{4},c_{4})=1\\
&&&d_{3}^{\hat{G}}(c_{3},c^{\prime}_{3})=1&\end{matrix}

Let us consider the
\mathcal{T}_{1}
-assignment
\varphi
and
\psi
between
F
and
\hat{G}
with
\varphi
given by

\begin{array}[]{llll}\lx@intercol\hfil\varphi_{i}(a_{i})=c_{i+1},\;i\in\{0,1,2\}\hfil\lx@intercol&\varphi_{3}(a_{3})=b^{\prime}_{4}\\
\varphi_{0}(b_{0})=c_{1}&\varphi_{1}(b_{1})=c^{\prime}_{2}&\varphi_{2}(b_{2})=b^{\prime}_{3}&\end{array}

and
\psi
sends everything to
a
, i.e.
\psi_{i}(\bullet_{i})=a_{i+1}
.

4.3
Optimization over complete linear orders

We assume in this section that
P
is a complete linear order.
Further, for simplicity we assume
(\varphi,\psi)
is a
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
-assignment instead of
\mathcal{S}
-assignment.
While the results are still valid for any
\mathcal{S}\in\mathrm{Flows}_{\mathcal{T}}
, we focus on
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
as we are interested in cases inducing interleavings from
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
-assignments.

Note that to obtain reduction formulas like in Corollary
4.7
, we need to work over discrete posets, however, many interesting cases arise from more general
P
.
In the next example, the density of
P=\mathbb{R}
allows us to choose assignments where the loss
L(\varphi,\psi)
is not finite.

Example 4.9
.

Consider
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}(m)=m+\varepsilon
from Example
3.5
.
Define the merge tress
F
and
G
in
\mathrm{Set}^{[0,\infty)}
by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F:}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{a_{t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(t)=\{a_{t}\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G:}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b^{0}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b^{0}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b^{0}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b^{0}_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b^{0}_{t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G(t)=\{b^{0}_{t}\}\cup\{b^{j}_{t}\}_{j=\lfloor t\rfloor+1}^{\infty}}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b^{1}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b^{2}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b^{2}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b_{0}^{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b_{1}^{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{b_{2}^{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}

Consider the
\mathcal{T}_{\frac{1}{2}}
-assignment
\varphi
and
\psi
given by

\varphi_{t}(a_{t})=\begin{cases}b^{0}_{t+\frac{1}{2}}&\text{if}\quad t=0\text{ or }t\geq 1\\
b^{n}_{t+\frac{1}{2}}&\text{if}\quad\dfrac{1}{2^{n+1}}\leq t<\dfrac{1}{2^{n}}\end{cases}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\psi_{t}(\bullet_{t})=a_{t+\frac{1}{2}}.

We can check that for
\frac{1}{2^{n+1}}\leq t<\frac{1}{2^{n}}
and
n>0

L_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.93002pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.03 6.3 L -3.78 6.3 L 0 0 L 5.81 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle\hbox to5.25pt{\vbox to3.59pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.11102pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.02 3.15 L -1.89 3.15 L 0 0 L 2.91 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}}^{0,t}(\varphi)=d_{t+\frac{1}{2}}^{G}\left(b^{0}_{t+\tfrac{1}{2}},b^{n}_{{t+\tfrac{1}{2}}}\right)=n-1+(1-(t+\tfrac{1}{2}))=n-t-\tfrac{1}{2}.

Since
G(0)\cong\mathbb{N}
, this means that there is a pair of elements with distance at least
N
for any
N
, so
L(\varphi,\psi)=\infty
.

Intuitively, the assignment fails because
\varphi
is not regular when it gets closer to 0, a critical value of
G
.
Hence, let us define assignments that are regular with respect to the functor.

.

.

Let
F,G\in C^{P}
be tame modules (Defn.
2.14
) with critical values for
F
and
G
given respectively by

c_{1}<\cdots<c_{m}

and
d_{1}<\cdots<d_{n}
.
Set
c_{0}=d_{0}=-\infty
and
c_{m+1}=d_{n+1}=\infty
to represent respectively the infimum and supremum elements of
P
.
The goal is to split the constant intervals
(c_{i},c_{i+1})
into intervals that map via
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
into constant intervals of
G
, and then to use the
\varphi
and
\psi
maps defined for representatives from the first piece of this decomposition to extend to values for the rest of the interval.

For
i\in\{0,\ldots,m\}
, consider the (possibly empty) intervals

\begin{matrix}J_{ij}=(c_{i},c_{i+1})\cap\{t\in P\mid\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t=d_{j}\}&j\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\\
J^{\prime}_{ij}=(c_{i},c_{i+1})\cap\{t\in P\mid\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t\in(d_{j},d_{j+1})\}&j\in\{0,\ldots,n\}.\end{matrix}

In the example of Figure
2
with
i=2
, we have broken up the interval
(c_{2},c_{3})
into three intervals:
J_{2,2}=(c_{2},c_{2}+1)
,
J^{\prime}_{2,2}=[c_{2}+1,c_{2}+3)
, and
J^{\prime}_{2,3}=[c_{2}+3,c_{3})
.

Choose one element from each non-empty
J_{ij}
or
J^{\prime}_{ij}
, and write these as
\{\alpha_{i,k}\mid k\in 1,\cdots,k_{i}\}
.
Again following the example of Figure
2
, we have

\alpha_{2,1}\in J_{2,2}
,

\alpha_{2,2}\in J_{2,2}^{\prime}
, and

\alpha_{2,3}\in J_{2,3}^{\prime}
.

To simplify notation, we reindex all the non-empty intervals and write
I_{i,k}
for the interval (whether of the form
J_{i,\cdot}
or
J^{\prime}_{i,\cdot}
) containing
\alpha_{i,k}
.
Note that
I_{i,k}\cap I_{i,k^{\prime}}=\emptyset
if
k\neq k^{\prime}
and
(c_{i},c_{i+1})=\bigcup_{k=1}^{k_{i}}I_{i,k}
.
Moreover, by Lemma
2.15
, the image of
I_{i,k}
under
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
is a constant interval of
G
.
Hence, we have divided
(c_{i},c_{i+1})
in intervals that map to constant intervals.
Let
R_{F}
be the points of
P
which are either chosen representatives or critical points
c_{i}
, specifically

R_{F}=\big\{\alpha_{i,k}\mid i\in\{0,\cdots,m\},k\in\{1,\cdots,k_{i}\}\big\}\cup\big\{c_{i}\mid i\in\{1,\cdots,m\}\big\}.

In a similar way, we can break up constant intervals of
G
of the form
(d_{j},d_{j+1})
into portions which map to constant intervals of
F
.
We then choose representatives
\beta_{j,\ell}
of these sub intervals, and define
R_{G}
to be

R_{G}=\big\{\beta_{j,\ell}\mid j\in\{0,\cdots,n\},\ell\in\{1,\cdots,\ell_{j}\}\big\}\cup\big\{d_{j}\mid j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}\big\}.

We will often use just the first representative in each interval, so we write
\alpha_{i}=\alpha_{i,1}
and
\beta_{j}=\beta_{j,1}
, and then write the relevant subsets of
R_{F}
and
R_{G}
as

\displaystyle B_{F}

\displaystyle=\big\{\alpha_{i}\mid i\in\{0,\cdots,m\}\big\}\cup\big\{c_{i}\mid i\in\{1,\cdots,m\}\big\},\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle B_{G}

\displaystyle=\big\{\beta_{j}\mid j\in\{0,\cdots,n\}\big\}\cup\big\{d_{j}\mid j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}\big\}.

Figure 2:
Example of the construction of the intervals
I_{i,k}
for the map
\mathcal{T}_{1}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
given by
\mathcal{T}_{1}(m)=\lfloor m\rfloor+1
and the functors
F:\mathbb{R}\to C
with critical set
\{c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}\}
and
G:\mathbb{R}\to C
with critical set
\{d_{1},d_{2},d_{3}\}
. For each interval we have
\alpha_{i,k}=I_{i,k}
.

.

We first need a simple lemma to simplify the proof.

Lemma 4.12
.

Take
F,G\in\mathcal{C}^{P}
and
(\varphi,\psi)
a constructible, Archimidean
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
-assignment between them.
Let
(c,c^{\prime})
to be a constant interval of
F
.
Then, for all

For the triangle terms we will show that for any
s^{\prime}\in P
we have
L_{\bigtriangledown}^{s^{\prime}}(\varphi,\psi)\leq\max_{s\in R_{F}}\{L_{\bigtriangledown}^{s}(\varphi,\psi)+\delta_{s}\}
which implies that this triangle term upper bounds the similar version in the original loss definition. Recall that

P=\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}\{c_{i}\}\cup\bigcup_{i=0}^{m}(c_{i},c_{i+1})=\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}\{c_{i}\}\cup\bigcup_{i=0}^{m}\bigcup_{k=1}^{k_{i}}I_{i,k}.

Then, for
s^{\prime}\in P
, the element
s^{\prime}
is either a critical point of
F
or
s^{\prime}\in I_{i,k}
for some interval
I_{i,k}
.
If
s^{\prime}
is a critical point, it is in the set
R_{F}
so we are done.
In the case where
s^{\prime}\in I_{i,k}
, by the definition of
I_{i,k}
and the fact that
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
is a constructible assignment we have that for any
x<y
in
I_{i,k}
,
L_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.93002pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}

and
L_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

.
Given that
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
is an Archimedean assignment, by Lemma
4.6
(2) there is
\delta>0
such that

L_{\bigtriangledown}^{y}(\varphi,\psi)\leq L_{\bigtriangledown}^{x}(\varphi,\psi)<L_{\bigtriangledown}^{y}(\varphi,\psi)+\delta.

Given that both
s^{\prime}
and
\alpha_{i,k}
are in
I_{i,k}
, this implies there is
\delta\geq 0
such that

L_{\bigtriangledown}^{s^{\prime}}(\varphi,\psi)<L_{\bigtriangledown}^{\alpha_{i,k}}(\varphi,\psi)+\delta.

Therefore, for any
s^{\prime}\in P
,

L_{\bigtriangledown}^{s^{\prime}}(\varphi,\psi)\leq\max_{s\in R_{F}}\{L_{\bigtriangledown}^{s}(\varphi,\psi)+\delta_{s}\}.

In the same way, using the critical values of
G
and
\beta_{j,\ell}
gives that

L_{\bigtriangleup}^{s^{\prime}}(\varphi,\psi)\leq\max_{s\in R_{G}}\{L_{\bigtriangleup}^{s}(\varphi,\psi)+\delta_{s}^{\prime}\}.

∎

Remark
.

The intervals
I_{i,k}
are of the form
\lfloor\inf I_{i,k},\inf I_{i,k+1}\rceil
or
\lfloor\inf I_{i,k},c_{i+1}\rceil
where the symbols
\lfloor
and
\rceil
denote that the lower and upper endpoints are either closed or open according to whether they are included in
I_{i,k}
, respectively. Moreover, if we choose
\alpha_{i,k}=\min I_{i,k}
when it exists we obtain that
\delta_{\alpha_{i,k}}=0
.

Creating a constructible assignment

Let us create a constructible
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
-assignment
(\varphi,\psi)
between
F
and
G
for given choices of maps on the sets
B_{F}
and
B_{G}
.
Specifically, assume we have chosen a collection of maps
\varphi_{r}:F(r)\to G\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}(r)
for
r\in B_{F}
.
Let us extend the maps to an unnatural transformation
\varphi:F\mathbin{\hskip 2.15277pt\scalebox{-1}[-1]{\hbox{\hskip 1.72221pt\hskip-1.72221pt\hbox{\raisebox{-1.6pt}{\uwave{\raisebox{2.0pt}{\hskip 3.44444pt}}}}\hskip-1.72221pt\hskip-1.72221pt\raisebox{-3.7pt}{\hbox{\raisebox{-1.6pt}{\uwave{\raisebox{2.0pt}{\hskip 3.44444pt}}}}}\hskip-1.72221pt\hskip 1.72221pt}}\mkern-2.6mu\scalebox{.7}[1.1]{$\succ$}}G\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
by setting

\varphi_{t}=\begin{cases}\varphi_{t}&\text{if }t\in B_{F}\\
(G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}])^{-1}\circ\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}\circ F[t\leq\alpha_{i}]&\text{if }t\in(c_{i},\alpha_{i})\\
G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t]\circ\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}\circ(F[\alpha_{i}\leq t])^{-1}&\text{if }t\in(\alpha_{i},c_{i+1}).\end{cases}

Symmetrically, construct
\psi:G\mathbin{\hskip 2.15277pt\scalebox{-1}[-1]{\hbox{\hskip 1.72221pt\hskip-1.72221pt\hbox{\raisebox{-1.6pt}{\uwave{\raisebox{2.0pt}{\hskip 3.44444pt}}}}\hskip-1.72221pt\hskip-1.72221pt\raisebox{-3.7pt}{\hbox{\raisebox{-1.6pt}{\uwave{\raisebox{2.0pt}{\hskip 3.44444pt}}}}}\hskip-1.72221pt\hskip 1.72221pt}}\mkern-2.6mu\scalebox{.7}[1.1]{$\succ$}}F\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
from a collection of maps
\psi_{r}:G(r)\to F\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}(r)
for
r\in B_{G}
and extending it for all values of
t\in P
.
Note that we are using the fact that the intervals
(c_{i},c_{i+1})
and
(d_{j},d_{j+1})
are constant for
F
and
G
respectively in order for these maps to be well defined.

Taking
(\varphi,\psi)
we obtain a
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
assignment of
F
and
G
. For this assignment we obtain that if
t\in(c_{i},\alpha_{i})

G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}]\circ\varphi_{t}=G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}]\circ(G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}])^{-1}\circ\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}\circ F[t\leq\alpha_{i}]=\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}\circ F[t\leq\alpha_{i}].

(3)

Equivalently, if
t\in(\alpha_{i},c_{i+1})

\varphi_{t}\circ F[\alpha_{i}\leq t]=G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t]\circ\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}\circ(F[\alpha_{i}\leq t])^{-1}\circ F[\alpha_{i}\leq t]=G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t]\circ\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}.

(4)

Lemma 4.13
.

The above defined
(\varphi,\psi)
is a constructible
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
-assignment.

Proof.

Note that for any
I
which is a constant interval of
F
,
I\subseteq(c_{i},c_{i+1})
for some
i
.
We need to show that for
s<t
in
I
,
L_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.93002pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.03 6.3 L -3.78 6.3 L 0 0 L 5.81 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\textstyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.93002pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.03 6.3 L -3.78 6.3 L 0 0 L 5.81 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle\hbox to5.25pt{\vbox to3.59pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.11102pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.42 4.41 L -2.64 4.41 L 0 0 L 4.07 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptscriptstyle\hbox to3.87pt{\vbox to2.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.565pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.02 3.15 L -1.89 3.15 L 0 0 L 2.91 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}}^{s,t}(\varphi)=0
.
We have three cases to show that
G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}s\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t]\circ\varphi_{s}=\varphi_{t}\circ F[s\leq t]
.

•

If
s<t\leq\alpha_{i}
:

\displaystyle G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}]\circ G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}s\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t]\circ\varphi_{s}

\displaystyle=G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}s\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}]\circ\varphi_{s}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}\circ F[s\leq\alpha_{i}]

by Eqn.

(
3
)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}\circ F[t\leq\alpha_{i}]\circ F[s\leq t]

\displaystyle=G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}]\circ\varphi_{t}\circ F[s\leq t]

by Eqn.

(
3
)

Since
G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t,\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}]
is isomorphism,
G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t,\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}]
can be canceled from the left to obtain the result.

•

If
s<\alpha_{i}\leq t
.

\displaystyle G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}s\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t]\circ\varphi_{s}

\displaystyle=G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}s\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}]\circ G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i},\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t]\circ\varphi_{s}

\displaystyle=G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}s\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}]\circ\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}\circ F[s\leq\alpha_{i}]

Eqn.

(
3
)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{t}\circ F[\alpha_{i},t]\circ F[s\leq\alpha_{i}]

Eqn.

(
4
)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{t}\circ F[s\leq t]

•

If
\mathbf{\alpha_{i}\leq s<t}

\displaystyle G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}s\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t]\circ\varphi_{s}\circ F[\alpha_{i}\leq s]

\displaystyle=G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}s\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t]\circ G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}s]\circ\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}

Eqn.

(
4
)

\displaystyle=G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{i}\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}t]\circ\varphi_{\alpha_{i}}

\displaystyle=\varphi_{t}\circ F[\alpha_{i}\leq t]

Eqn.

(
4
)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{t}\circ F[s\leq t]\circ F[\alpha_{i}\leq s]

Since
F[\alpha_{i}\leq s]
is isomorphism,
F[\alpha_{i}\leq s]
can be canceled from the right to obtain the result.

∎

4.4
Optimization over products of complete linear orders

Let us generalize the previous result for modules over
P^{k}
, the
k
-th product of the linear poset
P
. Take
F,G
tame modules of
C^{P^{k}}
with a
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
-assignment between them that is constructible and line-preserving.
The generalization has the following steps:

1.

Define the constant intervals of
P^{k}
with respect to
F,G
.

2.

Subdivide each constant interval of
F
into intervals that map into constant intervals of
G
via
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
( and viceversa).

3.

Define a poset of representatives of the intervals.

4.

Prove the loss is bounded by the loss of the set of representatives.

4.4.1
Defining constant intervals on
P^{k}

Let
F
,
G
in
C^{P^{k}}
be cubical tame modules (Def.
2.17
) and let

x^{i}_{2}<x^{i}_{4}<\cdots<x^{i}_{2m_{i}}

be the critical
i
-coordinates of
F
.
Similarly, let
y^{i}_{2}<\cdots<y^{i}_{2n_{i}}
be the critical
i
-coordinates of
G
.
Set
x^{i}_{0}=y^{i}_{0}=-\infty
and
x_{2(m+1)}=y_{2(n+1)}=\infty
where
-\infty
and
\infty
represent respectively the infimum and supremum elements of
P
.

First, we split
P^{k}
into constant intervals of
F
.
Consider the set of indices

\Lambda=\{(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})\mid\text{for }0\leq j_{i}\leq 2m_{i}+1\}.

For each
I=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})
in
\Lambda
, define the cube
S_{I}=X^{1}_{j_{1}}\times\cdots\times X^{k}_{j_{k}}
where
X^{i}_{j}=\{x^{i}_{j}\}
if
j
is even and
X^{i}_{j}=(x^{i}_{j-1},x^{i}_{j+1})
if
j
is odd.
For example, in Figure
3
, the cube
S_{33}=(x^{1}_{2},x^{1}_{4})\times(x^{2}_{2},x^{2}_{4})
, meanwhile
S_{32}=(x^{1}_{2},x^{1}_{4})\times\{x^{2}_{2}\}
.

Figure 3:
On the left: Example of the construction of the cubes
S_{(j_{1},j_{2})}=X^{1}_{j_{1}}\times X^{2}_{j_{2}}
for the functors
F:P^{2}\to C
with critical coordinates
\{x^{1}_{2},x^{1}_{4}\}
and
\{x^{2}_{2},x^{2}_{4}\}
. On the right: We divide the cube
S_{33}
into interval
\mathcal{I}_{J}^{33}
that map into the constant cube
R_{J}
by the morphism
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
. For example,
p\in\mathcal{I}_{12}^{33}
if and only if
p\in S_{33}
and
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p\in R_{12}
.

Theorem 4.14
.

The cubes
S_{I}\subseteq P^{k}
are constant intervals of
F
.

Proof.

Fix
I=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})\in\Lambda
.
Since each
X^{i}_{j}
is a convex and connected subset of
P
we have that
S_{I}
is a convex and connected subset of
P^{k}
, hence is an interval.
We will show that
F[p\leq q]
is an isomorphism for any
p\leq q\in S_{I}
and thus
S_{I}
is constant.
If all
j_{i}
’s are even,
S_{I}
is a point and
F
is constant on a point so we are done.
Thus assume
I
has at least one odd index and take
p<q\in S_{I}
.

We will do induction on the number of differing entries of
p
and
q
.
If
p
and
q
share all coordinates but one, we can write

p=\langle{p}^{1},\ldots,{p}^{k}\rangle
and

q=\langle p^{1},\ldots,p^{i-1},q^{i},p^{i+1},\ldots,p^{k}\rangle

with
p^{i}<q^{i}
and
j_{i}
odd, as denoted below:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle p^{1},\ldots,p^{i-1},q^{i},p^{i+1},\ldots,p^{k}\rangle}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle{p}^{1},\ldots,{p}^{k}\rangle}.}

If
F[p\leq q]
is not an isomorphism, then there is a critical value

\langle p^{1},\ldots,p^{i-1},t,p^{i+1},\ldots,p^{k}\rangle

of
F(\langle p^{1},\ldots,p^{i-1},\cdot,p^{i+1},\ldots,p^{k}\rangle):P\to C

with
x^{i}_{j_{i}-1}<p_{i}\leq t\leq q_{i}<x^{i}_{j_{i}+1}
.
However, the latter statement
contradicts the exhaustive list of critical
i
-coordinates.
Hence
F[p\leq q]
is isomorphism.

Now assume
p
and
q
differ in
n
coordinates, and assume that any points differing in
n-1
coordinates result in an isomorphism.
Write
p=\langle{p}^{1},\ldots,{p}^{k}\rangle\leq\langle{q}^{1},\ldots,{q}^{k}\rangle=q
in
S_{I}
let
i
be one of the coordinates that differ between the two.
Without loss of generality, assume
p^{i}<q^{i}
and set

r=\langle p^{1},\ldots,p^{i-1},q^{i},p^{i+1},\ldots,p^{k}\rangle.

so we have the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle p^{1},\ldots,p^{i-1},q^{i},p^{i+1},\ldots,p^{k}\rangle}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle{q}^{1},\ldots,{q}^{k}\rangle}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle{p}^{1},\ldots,{p}^{k}\rangle}}

By induction,
F[p\leq r]
and
F[r\leq q]
are isomorphisms.

Then, since
F[p\leq q]=F[r\leq q]\circ F[p\leq r]
we know that
F[p\leq q]
is an isomorphism.
Therefore,
F
is constant on
S_{I}
.
∎

We note that the indices in
\Lambda
used to enumerate the
S_{I}
are built to be compatible with the ordering of the
P^{k}
.
We make this explicit in the following lemma.

Lemma 4.15
.

The product order on
\Lambda\subseteq\mathbb{N}^{k}
used to index the sets
S_{I}
is compatible with the order of
P^{k}
. Explicitly, the following statements hold:

1.

If
p\leq q\in P^{k}
with
p\in S_{I}
and
q\in S_{I^{\prime}}
, then
I\leq_{\mathbb{N}^{k}}I^{\prime}
.

2.

If
I\leq_{\mathbb{N}^{k}}I^{\prime}
and
q\in S_{I^{\prime}}
, there is a
p\in S_{I}
such that
p\leq q
.

Proof.

(a)
Let
I=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})
and
I^{\prime}=(j^{\prime}_{1},\dots,j^{\prime}_{k})
be given elements in
\Lambda
.
On one hand assume that
p\leq q\in P^{k}
with
p\in S_{I}
and
q\in S_{I^{\prime}}
.
Then, for each
1\leq i\leq k
,
p^{i}\in X^{i}_{j_{i}}
and
q^{i}\in X^{i}_{j^{\prime}_{i}}
and
p^{i}\leq q^{i}
.
By construction of the
X_{j}
’s we can check that this implies that
j_{i}\leq j^{\prime}_{i}
.

(b)
Assume that
I\leq I^{\prime}
and choose
q\in S_{I}^{\prime}
.
If
I=I^{\prime}
we are done since
q
is comparable to itself.
Hence, suppose that
I<I^{\prime}
and take
p\in S_{I}
. If
q<p
.
By part
(a)
,
I^{\prime}\leq I
, but this contradicts the assumption that
I<I^{\prime}
.
So if
p
and
q
are comparable, then
p\leq q
as desired.

If
p
and
q
are not comparable, consider the non-empty sets

U=\{i\mid p^{i}\leq q^{i}\}
and
V=\{i\mid p^{i}>q^{i}\}
.
Define
r\in P^{k}
with
r^{i}=p^{i}
if
i\in U
and
r^{i}=q^{i}
if
i\in V
.
By construction we have that
r<p
and
r<q
as shown in the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle{q}^{1},\ldots,{q}^{k}\rangle}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle{r}^{1},\ldots,{r}^{k}\rangle}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\langle{p}^{1},\ldots,{p}^{k}\rangle}.}

Let
I^{\prime\prime}\in\Lambda
be such that
r\in S_{I^{\prime\prime}}
; we will show that
I^{\prime\prime}=I
, and thus
r\in S_{I}
which completes the proof.
If
i\in U
then
j_{i}^{\prime\prime}=j_{i}
because
X^{i}_{j_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\ni r^{i}=p^{i}\in X^{i}_{j_{i}}
.
Similarly, for
i\in V
we have that
j_{i}^{\prime\prime}=j_{i}^{\prime}
given that
X^{i}_{j_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\ni r^{i}=q^{i}\in X^{i}_{j_{i}^{\prime}}
.
Now, given that
r<p
and by part
(a)
, this implies that
I^{\prime\prime}\leq I
.
Hence, if
i\in V
,

j_{i}\leq j^{\prime}_{i}=j_{i}^{\prime\prime}\leq j_{i}
.
Therefore,
j_{i}^{\prime\prime}=j_{i}
if
i\in V
as well, so
I^{\prime\prime}=I
. ∎

Note that the collection
\{S_{I}\mid I\in\Lambda\}
covers all of
P^{k}
.
We will call
\{S_{I}\mid I\in\Lambda\}
the set of constant cubes of
F
.
We can define similarly
\Lambda^{\prime}
for the indices of the critical values of
G
and write
\{R_{J}\mid J\in\Lambda^{\prime}\}
for the set of constant cubes of
G
of the form
Y^{1}_{j_{1}}\times\cdots\times Y^{k}_{j_{k}}
.

4.4.2
Subdividing into intervals that map to constant intervals

Next, we will subdivide each
S_{I}
into intervals that map into constant intervals of
G
via
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
.
For each
I\in\Lambda
and
J\in\Lambda^{\prime}
, define

\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}=S_{I}\cap\{p\in P^{k}\mid\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p\in R_{J}\}.

Analogously, we define

\mathcal{J}_{I}^{J}=R_{J}\cap\{p\in P^{k}\mid\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p\in S_{I}\}

for the subdivision of the constant intervals of
G
mapping into constant intervals of
F
via
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
.

Remark
.

Because we assumed that
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
is line-preserving,
\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
can be written only in terms of terms of the form
\inf\mathcal{I}_{J^{\prime}}^{I}
as follows.
Consider
U=\{1\leq i\leq k\mid p^{i}\neq q^{i}\text{ for some }p,q\in\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}\}
.
Let
I=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})
and
J=(\hat{j}_{1},\dots,\hat{j}_{k})
.
For each
i\in U
take the index

J_{i}=(\hat{j}_{1},\dots,\hat{j}_{i-1},\hat{j}_{i}+1,\hat{j}_{i+1},\dots,\hat{j}_{k}).

Then

\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\inf\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}&\text{ if }U=\emptyset\\
\displaystyle\bigtimes_{i\in U}\left\lfloor\>(\inf\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I})^{i},(\inf\mathcal{I}_{J_{i}}^{I})^{i}\>\right\rceil&\text{ if }U\neq\emptyset\text{ and }\inf\mathcal{I}_{J_{i}}^{I}\neq\emptyset\\
\displaystyle\bigtimes_{i\in U}\left\lfloor\>(\inf\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I})^{i},x^{i}_{j_{i}+1}\>\right\rceil&\text{ if }U\neq\emptyset\text{ and }\inf\mathcal{I}_{J_{i}}^{I}=\emptyset\end{array}\right.

Where
(a)^{i}
denotes the
i
-th coordinate of
a\in P^{k}
. The symbols
\lfloor
and
\rceil
denote that the lower and upper endpoints are closed or open according to whether they are included in
\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
, respectively.
Hence, when
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
is line-preserving the intervals
\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
and
\mathcal{J}_{I}^{J}
are cubes.

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
I\leq I^{\prime}\in\Lambda
and
J<J^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
, and suppose that

\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
and
\mathcal{I}_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}}
are non-empty.
Then, for each
q\in\mathcal{I}_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}}
there is
p\in\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
with
p\leq q
.

Proof.

Assume that
J=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})<(j^{\prime}_{1},\dots,j^{\prime}_{k})=J^{\prime}
.
Let
q\in\mathcal{I^{\prime}}_{J^{\prime}}^{I}
and
p\in\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
be given.
If
q<p
, then
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}q\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p
, but this contradicts the assumption that
J<J^{\prime}
. So if
p
and
q
are comparable, then
p\leq q
as desired.

If
p
and
q
are not comparable, define
r
using the sets
U
and
V
as in the proof of Lemma
4.15
(b).
Since
p\in S_{I}
,
r\in S_{I}
by the lemma.
Let
J^{\prime\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}
be such that
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}r\in R_{J^{\prime\prime}}
, so by construction,
r\in\mathcal{I}_{J^{\prime\prime}}^{I}
.

Since
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
is line-preserving and by using Lemma
2.11
, for each
1\leq i\leq k
there are translations
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}^{i}\colon P\to P
such that

\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}r=(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}^{1}(r^{1}),\ldots,\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}^{k}(r^{k})).

This implies that if
i\in U
,
(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}(r))^{i}=(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}(p))^{i}
; and if
i\in V
,
(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}(r))^{i}=(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}(q))^{i}
.
Hence, by the same argument from Lemma
4.15
, we have that
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}r\in R_{J}
.
Thus
r\in\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
completing the proof.
∎

4.4.3
Choosing representatives for each interval

In Section
4.3
where we studied modules over
P=P^{1}
, we could choose a representative
\alpha_{i,k}
in the constant intervals of
F
that map to constant intervals of
G
since all elements in these intervals are comparable to their representative.
However, when we have modules over
P^{k}
for
k\geq 2
, this is no longer the case since we can find elements in a cube
I
that are not comparable to each other.
For example, consider
I=(0,1)\times(0,1)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
For all
\langle x,y\rangle\in I
there is
\varepsilon>0
such that
\langle x-\varepsilon,y+\varepsilon\rangle\in I
and
\langle x-\varepsilon,y+\varepsilon\rangle
is not comparable to
\langle x,y\rangle
.
Therefore, we need a different strategy for finding representatives in
P^{k}
.

For a given flow
\mathcal{T}
over
P^{k}
, the next definition gives a poset
\hat{P}
by constructing symbols
\alpha^{I}_{J}
that will be used to represent each
\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
.
Specifically, the elements of
\hat{P}
will be written as
\mathcal{S}\alpha^{I}_{J}
for each
\mathcal{S}\in\mathrm{Flows}_{\mathcal{T}}
(Def.
2.7
).
While the actual evaluation of
\mathcal{S}
on
\alpha^{I}_{J}
is not defined since
\mathcal{T}
is not defined over
\hat{P}
, only over
P^{k}
, we will use it to symbolically represent elements that get mapped to the inverse limit of the constant intervals.

Definition 4.17
.

Let

\hat{P}=\{\mathcal{S}\alpha^{I}_{J}\mid\mathcal{I}^{I}_{J}\neq\emptyset\text{ and }\mathcal{S}\in\mathrm{Flows}_{\mathcal{T}}\}
.
Define the relation
\leq
in
\hat{P}
by the following:

•

If
I\leq I^{\prime}
and
J\leq J^{\prime}
, for all
\mathcal{S}\in\mathrm{Flows}_{\mathcal{T}}
,
then
\mathcal{S}\alpha^{I}_{J}\leq\mathcal{S}\alpha^{I^{\prime}}_{J^{\prime}}
.

•

For all
\mathcal{S}\leq\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Flows}_{\mathcal{T}}
,
\mathcal{S}\alpha^{I}_{J}\leq\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\alpha^{I}_{J}
.

Note that for ease of notation, we will write the element
\alpha^{I}_{J}=\mathrm{Id}\;\alpha^{I}_{J}
.
Also, since
\mathrm{Id}\in\mathcal{S}
, the first assumption implies that if
I\leq I^{\prime}
and
J\leq J^{\prime}
, then
\alpha^{I}_{J}\leq\alpha^{I^{\prime}}_{J^{\prime}}
.

With this construction, we define the map
\hat{F}:\hat{P}\to C
by using the inverse limit, explicitly

\hat{F}(\mathcal{S}\alpha^{I}_{J})=\varprojlim(F(\mathcal{S}p)\mid p\in\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}).

If the index
I
is clear by context or not necessary we write
\alpha_{J}
instead of
\alpha^{I}_{J}
.

We next show that this map is indeed functorial.

Theorem 4.18
.

The map
\hat{F}:\hat{P}\to C
is a functor.

Proof.

First, we need to prove that for
\hat{p}\leq\hat{q}
in
\hat{P}
,
\hat{F}(\hat{p})\to\hat{F}(\hat{q})
exists.
Let us start by finding the map
F(\alpha_{J}^{I})\to F(\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}})
when
I\leq I^{\prime}
and
J\leq J^{\prime}
.
Recall that in Lemma
4.16
, if we take
q\in\mathcal{I}_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}}
there is
p\in\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
with
p\leq q
.
Take
q\leq q^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{I}_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}}
, then
p\leq p^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
.
Given that
\hat{F}(\alpha_{J}^{I})
is the inverse limit and following the diagram on the left of Fig.
4
we have that

F[p^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}]\circ(F[p\leq p^{\prime}]\circ F[\alpha_{J}\leq p])=F[p^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}]\circ(F[\alpha_{J}\leq p^{\prime}]).

By the Thin Lemma we know that

F[p^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}]\circ F[p\leq p^{\prime}]=F[q\leq q^{\prime}]\circ F[p\leq q]
.
Plugging this equality on the left side of the equation yields

F[q\leq q^{\prime}]\circ F[p\leq q]\circ F[\alpha_{J}\leq p]=F[p^{\prime}\leq q^{\prime}]\circ F[\alpha_{J}\leq p^{\prime}].

Setting
\gamma_{q}=F[p\leq q]\circ F[\alpha_{J}\leq p]
, this is the same as writing

F[q\leq q^{\prime}]\circ\gamma_{q}=\gamma_{q}^{\prime}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\hat{F}(\alpha_{J}^{I})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\hat{F}(\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(q)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(q^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(p)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(p^{\prime})}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\hat{F}(\mathcal{S}\alpha_{J})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\hat{F}(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\alpha_{J})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(\mathcal{S}p)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(\mathcal{S}p^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}p)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{F(\mathcal{S}^{\prime}p^{\prime})}}

Figure 4:
Diagrams for defining
\hat{F}
in the proof of Thm.
4.18
.

Hence, by universal property of
\hat{F}(\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}})
we obtain a unique morphism
\hat{F}(\alpha_{J}^{I})\to\hat{F}(\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}})
that gives rise to
\hat{F}[\alpha_{J}^{I}\leq\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I}]
. Moreover, from this property we obtain that

F[p\leq q]\circ F[\alpha_{J}^{I}\leq p]=F[\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}}\leq q]\circ\hat{F}[\alpha_{J}^{I}\leq\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}}]

(5)

for any
p\leq q
with in
p\in\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
and
q\in\mathcal{I}_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}}
. By a similar argument, for any
\mathcal{S}\in\mathrm{Flows}_{\mathcal{T}}
, we can find the morphisms
\hat{F}[\mathcal{S}\alpha_{J}^{I}\leq\mathcal{S}\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}}]
.

Similarly for all for all
\mathcal{S}\leq\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Flows}_{\mathcal{T}}
we have the morphisms
\hat{F}[\mathcal{S}\alpha_{J}\leq\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\alpha_{J}]
by the universal property of
F(\mathcal{S^{\prime}}\alpha_{J})
as shown in the diagram on the right of Fig.
4
.
Additionally, the uniqueness from the universal property ensures that
\hat{F}
is compatible with the identity morphism and the associative property of the composition.
∎

Symmetrically, we define the functor
\hat{G}:\hat{P}\to C
given by

\hat{G}(\mathcal{S}\alpha^{I}_{J})=\varprojlim(G(\mathcal{S}p)\mid p\in\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I})

with the canonical morphism
G[\mathcal{S}\alpha^{I}_{J}\leq\mathcal{S}p]:\hat{G}(\mathcal{S}\alpha^{I}_{J})\to G(\mathcal{S}p)
.

Note that for each
p\in\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
we have a canonical morphism
\hat{F}(\mathcal{S}\alpha^{I}_{J})\to F(\mathcal{S}p)
.
We will abuse notation and call this morphism
F[\mathcal{S}\alpha^{I}_{J}\leq\mathcal{S}p]
.

Assume we are given
(\varphi,\psi)
, a constructible
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
-assignment between
F
and
G
, defined by
\varphi:F\to G\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
and
\psi:F\to G\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
.
Our next goal is to "expand" these collection to
\varphi_{\alpha_{J}}:\hat{F}(\alpha_{J})\to\hat{G}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J})
and

\psi_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J}}:\hat{G}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J})\to\hat{F}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J})
for all
\alpha_{J}^{I}
in
\hat{P}
.

Consider the diagram on the left of Figure
5
where
p\leq q\in\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
.
We can verify that the triangle with corners
\hat{F}(\alpha_{J})
,
F(p)
, and
F(q)
commutes since
\hat{F}(\alpha_{J})
is the inverse limit of
F
.
By assumption,
F
is constant on
\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}\subseteq S_{I}
and thus the bottom square commutes; equivalently
L_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.93002pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

.
Thus the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{F}(\alpha_{J})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}(p)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}(q)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{p}\circ F[\alpha_{J}\leq p]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{q}\circ F[\alpha_{J}\leq q]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}q]}

commutes, making it a cone.
But since
\hat{G}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J})
is a inverse limit, this means there is a unique map

\varphi_{\alpha_{J}}\colon\hat{F}(\alpha_{j})\to\hat{G}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J})
making the left diagram of Fig.
4
commute, i.e. with the property that

\varphi_{p}\circ F[\alpha_{J}\leq p]=G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J}\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p]\circ\varphi_{\alpha_{J}}.

(6)

Symmetrically, we can define
\psi
in such a way that the diagram on the right of Figure
5
commutes.
Hence, the following equation holds:

\psi_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p}\circ G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J}\leq\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}p]=F[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J}\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{T}_{\epsilon}p]\circ\psi_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J}}

(7)

In this way, for
\alpha_{J}^{I}\leq\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}}
we can define the parallelogram diagram

\hbox to10.3pt{\vbox to6.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.10004pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

as well as the triangle diagram

\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\bigtriangledown$}_{\varphi,\psi}(\alpha_{J}^{I})
in the same manner as in Sec.
3.2
.

Diagrams that show the existence of the maps
\hat{F}(\alpha_{J})\to\hat{G}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J})
and

by the universal property.

Note that all this work has been done to define
\varphi
and
\psi
on
\hat{P}
, i.e. constant intervals of
F
that map to constant intervals of
G
.
However, we need all this information for constant intervals of
G
that map to constant intervals of
F
.
Thus, in a similar fashion, we will define

\tilde{P}=\{\mathcal{S}{\beta}^{I}_{J}\mid\mathcal{J}_{I}^{J}\text{ and }\mathcal{S}\in\mathrm{Flows}_{\mathcal{T}}\}

on symbols
\beta_{J}^{I}
with similar order relations to
\hat{P}
.
Further, define
\tilde{G}\colon\tilde{P}\to C
and
\tilde{F}\colon\tilde{P}\to C
by

\tilde{G}(\mathcal{S}\beta_{I}^{J})=\lim_{\longleftarrow}\left(G(\mathcal{S}p)\mid p\in\mathcal{J}_{I}^{J}\right)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\tilde{F}(\mathcal{S}\beta_{I}^{J})=\lim_{\longleftarrow}\left(F(\mathcal{S}p)\mid p\in\mathcal{J}_{I}^{J}\right).

Using the same arguments, both
\tilde{G}
and
\tilde{F}
are functors. We also have the maps
\psi_{\beta_{I}}\colon\tilde{G}(\beta_{I})\to\tilde{F}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\beta_{I})
and
\psi_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\beta_{I}}\colon\tilde{F}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\beta_{I})\to\tilde{G}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\beta_{I})
.
Putting all these together, we have diagrams as before: the parallelogram diagram
\hbox to10.3pt{\vbox to6.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

and the triangle diagram
\bigtriangleup_{\varphi,\psi}(\beta_{I}^{J})
.

4.4.4
Loss function of the representatives set

Notice that even though we have not defined a flow
\mathcal{T}
on
\hat{P}
, we can still define a merging distance (Defn.
3.1
)

d^{\hat{F}}_{\hat{p}}

on
\hat{F}(\hat{p})

for
\hat{p}\in\hat{P}
given by

d_{\hat{p}}^{\hat{F}}(a,b)=\inf\{\varepsilon\geq 0\mid\hat{F}[\hat{p}\leq T_{\varepsilon}\hat{p}](a)=\hat{F}[\hat{p}\leq T_{\varepsilon}\hat{p}](b)\}.

Similarly we have the distance
d^{\hat{G}}_{\hat{p}}
on
\hat{G}(\hat{p})
.

Lemma 4.19
.

For any

with

we have

Proof.

Figure 6:
Diagrams for the proof of Lem.
4.19

Fix
p\leq q
with
p\in\mathcal{I}_{J}^{I}
and
q\in\mathcal{I}_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}}
, which implies
\alpha_{J}^{I}\leq\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}}
.
Consider the diagram at left of Fig.
6
with element chase given on the right.
The parallelogram formed only with versions of
F
commutes by
Eq. (
5
).
A similar argument gives that the parallelogram formed by versions of
G
also commutes.
The left and right parallelograms, namely

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{F}(\alpha_{J}^{I})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{G}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J}^{I})}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\alpha_{J}^{I}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{p}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{F}(\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{G}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}q)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{q}}

commute by Eqn. (
6
) when

\varphi_{\alpha_{J}^{I}}
and
\varphi_{\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I^{\prime}}}
are defined.
The two remaining parallelograms are
\hbox to10.3pt{\vbox to6.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 4.10004pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.91 8.99 L -5.4 8.99 L 0 0 L 8.3 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\varphi}(p,q)
and

, which we call the back and front respectively.

Then, consider
a^{\prime}\in F(p)
and
a=F[\alpha_{J}\leq p]^{-1}(a^{\prime})
and follow respectively their images on the back and front parallelograms as labeled in the right of Fig.
6
.
We have that
d_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}q}^{G}(c,c^{\prime})\leq d_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J^{\prime}}}^{\hat{G}}(b,b^{\prime})
by Lemma
3.6
, so by definition of the loss this implies that

L_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.93002pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.03 6.3 L -3.78 6.3 L 0 0 L 5.81 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle\hbox to5.25pt{\vbox to3.59pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.11102pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.42 4.41 L -2.64 4.41 L 0 0 L 4.07 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptscriptstyle\hbox to3.87pt{\vbox to2.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.565pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.03 6.3 L -3.78 6.3 L 0 0 L 5.81 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\textstyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.93002pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.42 4.41 L -2.64 4.41 L 0 0 L 4.07 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptscriptstyle\hbox to3.87pt{\vbox to2.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.565pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.02 3.15 L -1.89 3.15 L 0 0 L 2.91 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}}^{\alpha_{J},\alpha_{J^{\prime}}}(\varphi)

as required.

Now, let us bound the triangle loss.
Consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{F}(\alpha_{J})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(p)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{F}(\alpha_{J^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(p^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{G}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F[\alpha_{J}\leq p]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\alpha_{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F[\leq]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F[\alpha_{J^{\prime}}\leq p^{\prime}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p}}

where we have set
p^{\prime}=\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p
and
\alpha_{J}^{\prime}=\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J}
.
The left parallelogram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{F}(\alpha_{J})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(p)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{G}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p)}

commutes by Eqn. (
6
), and the right parallelogram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{F}(\alpha_{J}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(p^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{G}(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\alpha_{J})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G(\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p)}

commutes by Eqn. (
7
).
Additionally,
F[\alpha^{I}_{J}\leq p]
is isomorphism because for all
p\leq q
in
\mathcal{I}^{I}_{J}\subset S_{I}
the morphism
F[p\leq q]
is an isomorphism. Then, by universal property it follows that
F[\alpha^{I}_{J}\leq p]
is isomorphism as well.
Since all conditions of Lemma
4.6
(1) are satisfied we can conclude that

L_{\bigtriangledown}^{p}(\varphi,\psi)\leq L_{\bigtriangledown}^{\alpha_{J}}(\varphi,\psi)

finishing the proof.
∎

Analogously, we can define the diagrams

\hbox to10.3pt{\vbox to6.9pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 13.7 8.99 L 5.4 8.99 L 0 0 L 8.3 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\psi}(\beta_{I}^{J},\beta_{I^{\prime}}^{J^{\prime}})
and

\bigtriangleup_{\varphi,\psi}(\beta_{I}^{J})

with the corresponding losses

L_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

.

Putting all of this infrastructure together and following the same argument as Thm.
4.11
, we have the final main theorem.

Theorem 4.20
.

Let
F
,
G
in
C^{P}
be cubical tame and
(\varphi,\psi)
be a given constructible, line-preserving
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
-assignment between them.
Then, the loss is given by

L(\varphi,\psi)=\max\left\{\max_{\hat{p}\prec\hat{q}\;\in\hat{B}}\{L_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.93002pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.03 6.3 L -3.78 6.3 L 0 0 L 5.81 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\textstyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.93002pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.03 6.3 L -3.78 6.3 L 0 0 L 5.81 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle\hbox to5.25pt{\vbox to3.59pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.11102pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.42 4.41 L -2.64 4.41 L 0 0 L 4.07 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptscriptstyle\hbox to3.87pt{\vbox to2.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.565pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.02 3.15 L -1.89 3.15 L 0 0 L 2.91 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}}^{\hat{p},\hat{q}}(\varphi)\},\max_{\hat{p}\in\{\alpha_{J}^{I}\}}\{L_{\bigtriangledown}^{\hat{p}}(\varphi,\psi)\},\max_{\tilde{p}\prec\tilde{q}\;\in\tilde{B}}\{L_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 9.59 6.3 L 3.78 6.3 L 0 0 L 5.81 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\textstyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.71 4.41 L 2.64 4.41 L 0 0 L 4.07 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptscriptstyle\hbox to3.87pt{\vbox to2.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.79 3.15 L 1.89 3.15 L 0 0 L 2.91 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}}^{\tilde{p},\tilde{q}}(\psi)\},\max_{\tilde{p}\in\{\beta_{J}^{I}\}}\{L_{\bigtriangleup}^{\tilde{p}}(\varphi,\psi)\}\right\}

where

\hat{B}=\{\alpha_{J}^{I}\mid\alpha_{J}^{I}\text{ minimal in the poset }\{\alpha_{J^{\prime}}^{I}\mid\mathcal{I}_{J^{\prime}}^{I}\neq\emptyset,J^{\prime}\in\Lambda\}\}

and

\tilde{B}=\{\beta_{I}^{J}\mid\beta_{I}^{J}\text{ minimal in the poset }\{\beta_{I^{\prime}}^{J}\mid\mathcal{J}_{I^{\prime}}^{J}\neq\emptyset,I^{\prime}\in\Lambda^{\prime}\}\}.

Finally, we use this theorem to compute the loss for multiparameter persistence modules.
Consider
F,G:P^{k}\to\mathrm{Vec}_{\mathbb{F}}
to be cubical tame and
(\varphi,\psi)
a constructible
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
-assignment.
First we set
E
to be the maximum of the number of
i
-critical coordinates of
F
and
G
over all indices
i
.
Then, we define
N
to be the maximum difference in rank for all pairs of maps following around two sides of the relevant parallelogram and triangle diagrams for the loss function.
Explicitly, this is

\displaystyle N=\max\Bigg\{

\displaystyle\max_{\hat{p}\prec\hat{q}\;\in\hat{B}}\left\{\rk(\varphi_{\hat{q}}\circ\hat{F}[\hat{p}\leq\hat{q}]-\hat{G}[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\hat{p}\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\hat{q}]\circ\varphi_{\hat{p}})\right\},\max_{\hat{p}\in\{\alpha_{J}^{I}\}}\left\{\rk(\psi_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\hat{p}}\varphi_{\hat{p}}-\hat{F}[\hat{p}\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\hat{p}])\right\},

\displaystyle\max_{\tilde{p}\prec\tilde{q}\;\in\tilde{B}}\left\{\rk(\psi_{\tilde{q}}\circ\tilde{G}[\tilde{p}\leq\tilde{q}]-\tilde{F}[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\tilde{p}\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\tilde{q}]\circ\psi_{\tilde{p}})\right\},\max_{\tilde{p}\in\{{\beta}_{I}^{J}\}}\left\{\rk(\varphi_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\tilde{p}}\psi_{\tilde{p}}-\tilde{G}[\tilde{p}\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\tilde{p}])\right\}.\Bigg\}

We then define
M
as a bound for the number of reducing constants necessary to check, given explicitly as

M=\max\left\{\max_{\hat{p}\in\{\alpha_{J}^{I}\}}\left\{D^{\hat{G}}_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\hat{p}},D^{\hat{F}}_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\hat{p}}\right\},\max_{\hat{p}\in\{\beta_{I}^{J}\}}\left\{D^{\tilde{F}}_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\tilde{p}},D^{\tilde{G}}_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\tilde{p}}\right\}\right\}.

With these constants, we can bound the time to calculate the loss as follows.

Corollary 4.21
.

Consider
F,G:P^{k}\to Vec_{\mathbb{F}}
to be
q
-tame and cubical tame and
(\varphi,\psi)
a constructible
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
-assignment. The time complexity of calculating
L(\varphi,\psi)
is

O(E^{2k}N^{3}\log(M))

with
E
,
N
, and
M
given as above.

Proof.

From Algorithm
2
we know that the time required to calculate
L_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{.7}{$\displaystyle\hbox to7.33pt{\vbox to4.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.93002pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.03 6.3 L -3.78 6.3 L 0 0 L 5.81 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptstyle\hbox to5.25pt{\vbox to3.59pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.11102pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.42 4.41 L -2.64 4.41 L 0 0 L 4.07 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}{\scalebox{.7}{$\scriptscriptstyle\hbox to3.87pt{\vbox to2.68pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.565pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@roundjoin\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.02 3.15 L -1.89 3.15 L 0 0 L 2.91 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}}^{p,q}(\varphi)
is
O(n^{3}\log(m))
where
n=\rk(\varphi_{q}\circ F[p\leq q]-G[\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}q]\circ\varphi_{p})
and
m=|D^{G}_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p}|
.
By construction
n\leq N
and
m\leq M
.
We have a similar expression for

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.79 3.15 L 1.89 3.15 L 0 0 L 2.91 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$}}}^{p,q}(\varphi)
.
An analogous argument gives that the time required to calculate

L_{\bigtriangledown}^{p}(\varphi,\psi)

is
O(n^{\prime 3}\log(m^{\prime}))

where
n^{\prime}=\rk(F[p\leq\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p]-\psi_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p}\circ\varphi_{p})
}

and
m^{\prime}=|D^{F}_{\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}p}|
.
Again
n^{\prime}\leq N
and
m^{\prime}\leq M
.
Likewise, we have a expression for
L_{\bigtriangleup}^{p}(\varphi,\psi)
.
Thus,
O(N^{3}log(M))
bounds the required time to calculate the loss of any diagram.
Finally, by Thm.
4.20
, the number of diagrams that we need to check is bounded by

2|\{\alpha_{J}^{I}\}|+2|\{{\beta}_{I}^{J}\}|
, and each cardinality is upper bounded by
(2E+1)^{2k}
.
∎

5
Discussion

In this paper, in the context of persistence modules valued in concrete categories indexed by a poset, we defined a loss function that measures how far an assignment (i.e. family of maps that resemble a natural transformation) is from being an interleaving.
We showed that this loss provides an upper bound for the interleaving distance between generalized persistence modules.
Then, we optimized the computation of the loss for persistence modules indexed by
P
pr
P^{k}
when
P
is a complete linear order.
Moreover, we gave polynomial time algorithms for the computation of the loss for persistence modules valued in categories whose objects have underlying finite sets or the category of vector spaces.

Our results open the possibility of an algorithmic approach for obtaining upper bounds on the interleaving distance for commonly used constructions, such as multiparameter persistence modules, for which the exact computation of the interleaving distance has been shown to be NP-complete
(
Bjerkevik & Botnan 2018
)
.
However, we emphasize that our approach does not guarantee a tight bound.
Hence, a natural direction for future work is the implementation of algorithms that search for the best possible bound over all possible assignments.
While this problem will inevitably be NP-hard, additional care in developing strategies to explore the space of assignments might yield experimentally practical results, as was done in the case of this loss function for mapper graphs using linear programming
(
Chambers et al. 2026
)
.

Furthermore, our framework applies to any structure that can be represented as a generalized persistence module, including merge trees
(
Morozov et al. 2013
)
, Reeb graphs
(
de Silva et al. 2016
)
, Mapper graphs
(
Chambers et al. 2025
)
, sheaves and cosheaves in concrete categories.
Consequently, the proposed loss yields an upper bound on the interleaving distance between such structures.
The development of algorithms to compute this loss requires further work and is inherently context dependent, relying on the specific properties of each structure; for example, the existence of basis elements when the indexing poset consists of collections of open sets
(
Chambers et al. 2025
)
.

There are some immediate generalizations for our work which would be interesting for future work.
One can replace the notion of
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
-interleaving by weak
\mathcal{T}_{\varepsilon}
-interleaving
(
de Silva et al. 2018
)
and use a more general metric.
Another route is exploring the interleaving distance of persistence modules defined in non-concrete such as the category of topological spaces.
The definition of our loss function is based on a distance between elements inside an object of the category.
Thus, the key is finding a definition of a loss function that is independent of the content of the object.
Finally, we optimize the computation of the loss for persistence modules indexed by
P^{k}
when
P
is a complete linear order, due to the applications to multiparameter persistence that we were interested in.
In future work, we would like to explore such optimization when the poset
P
has other properties like modularity and latticity.
For instance, sheaves and cosheaves are naturally indexed by distributive lattices.
